## 16. Slice C — the Product Owner's UX correction (2026-11-15; BINDING; applied inside Slice C, not a redesign)

### 16.1 Source and standing

Two PO messages, received after the first Slice C HONOR captures (commit eecd954f, `proof/eecd954f/_c1 … _c6.png`): "COSMOS-INT-01 — PO UX Decision: Simplify Navigation Chrome & Timeline Labels in Slice C", and the follow-up «وأيضاً الأزرار في أماكنها في النسختين العربية والإنجليزية — لا تبديل للأماكن اليمين واليسار» (the controls keep their places in both language versions; no swapping of right and left). The PO's finding: the upper navigation area of the first Slice C screen is overcrowded — the T-07 acts as a stack of floating text chips under the Overview / Back row re-introduce the old QANDEEL layout; COSMOS was selected for its full-screen experience, compact intelligent controls and minimal wasted space. The message is "an authorized Slice C UX correction, not permission to redesign COSMOS"; it does not start Slice D, change privacy or authority contracts, or permit a merge. It binds over §15.3 rows B (the acts' placement) and the temporal line row where they differ.

### 16.2 The decisions (U-1 … U-9)

| ID | Decision (PO, binding) |
|---|---|
| U-1 | The upper screen ordinarily holds no more than three navigation controls: **Conversation** (the application's existing top band; returns to the Conversation depth; the established depth navigation), **Overview** (compact; presentation-only; never changes the canonical camera or the temporal position) and **Back** (the accepted COSMOS behaviour: one semantic level OUT per press, shown contextually; at WORLD the approved return-to-centre where applicable). |
| U-2 | No separate, permanently visible "Return to the whole world" button beside Overview. The two acts stay semantically distinct (Overview = temporary presentation-only framing; Return to the whole world = authorised canonical navigation). The canonical return stays available through a compact secondary / contextual affordance, preferably associated with Overview. Never silently combined; Overview's accepted behaviour unchanged. |
| U-3 | The duplicate "Rejoin the conversation and move there" control leaves the upper map chrome. The authorised return-to-live and temporal actions belong in the compact bottom timeline area. The distinctions between opening Conversation, returning to the Live Edge, returning to Live Focus and the historical exact return are preserved; no T-07 / T-08 function is discarded; no duplicated control is introduced. |
| U-4 | The old floating navigation-button stack, the redundant explanatory paragraphs and their reserved layout space are removed and the space reclaimed for the cosmic world — not replaced with another crowded toolbar. Preserved: the COSMOS artwork, lighting, colours and motion; the approved Overview and one-level Back; nearest-topic navigation; the corrected pinch and double tap; the zoom-level rail permanently on the RIGHT in Arabic and English. |
| U-5 | The COSMOS segmented / dashed bottom rail stays visually unchanged and carries the real T-05 / T-06 functions (disclosed Moments and the historical position; preview and scrub; commit and cancel; beginning, horizon and end; long-history windowing; return to the live edge). No further large timeline toolbar, no unnecessary textual rows. Any unavoidable visible design departure requires PO approval. |
| U-6 | **The Moment label above the rail loses its prefix:** "Reading at moment 12." → "Moment 12"; Arabic the natural equivalent (the PO wrote «اللحظة ١٢»), following QANDEEL's existing locale and numbering policy rather than hard-coding numeral shapes. Only the concise label and the ACTUAL committed Moment number; no "Reading at" / «القراءة عند» or any explanatory prefix; no trailing full stop; always the real temporal position, never a fixture; the application's language; correct RTL / LTR alignment and typography; compact, above the approved rail; the rail not enlarged, no new row of explanatory text; the T-05 / T-06 semantics and accessibility information preserved; at the Live Edge, or any state with no committed historical Moment, the existing authorised temporal state is used — no Moment number is invented. A copy and presentation simplification, not a change of the temporal-navigation model. |
| U-7 | Every change works in Arabic and English: the existing application locale, no independent language switch, correct reading direction and natural Arabic wording, English LTR, proper alignment and spacing, the zoom-level rail on the RIGHT in both, native RTL mirroring must not clip or reverse the approved COSMOS controls, compact Arabic labels fit without clipping or overlap on small Android screens, and any NEW user-visible wording goes through the existing Copy Gate. Arabic receives the same visual quality as English. |
| U-8 | Validation: record the decisions in the Slice C acceptance contract; identify the current upper controls and the distinct canonical acts they own; the smallest composition changes that reduce the clutter; verify Overview, one-level Back, Return World, Return Live and the other temporal acts independently; verify "Moment 12" / its Arabic form from the real temporal state in both languages; test Arabic RTL and English LTR including narrow screens; before / after HONOR screenshots of the upper controls, the timeline label and the reclaimed map area; COSMOS performance and the approved visual design preserved. Stop for PO review before any merge. |
| U-9 | **Control positions are identical in Arabic and English** — no right / left swapping between the two language versions (the follow-up message). |

**Final PO acceptance criteria (U-8):** minimal, uncluttered navigation controls; maximum usable screen area for COSMOS; the exact approved segmented timeline appearance; the compact Moment label without "Reading at"; correct Arabic and English presentation; no lost QANDEEL functionality.

### 16.3 How Slice C applies them (the smallest composition changes; engineering's choices inside the decisions, for the PO to confirm or redirect — none inferred as approved)

The upper controls of the first Slice C screen and the acts they owned: the host's **Conversation** return (top band, unchanged); the COSMOS **Back** chip (one level OUT; at WORLD away from the centre the T-07 `RETURN_WORLD`, §15.3 C); the COSMOS **Overview** chip (presentation only); and the contextual acts row under them — `BACK_ONE_STEP`, `EXACT_RETURN`, `RETURN_LIVE_FOCUS`, `GO_LIVE_AND_LOCATE`, `RETURN_WORLD` at a deeper rung, and T-04's context choice with its ordering note.

| ID | Application |
|---|---|
| A-1 | **Upper screen = Conversation, Back, Overview only** (U-1). The contextual acts row is removed from the top of the stage; the `acts` seam of the world view is retired. Back and Overview keep their accepted behaviour. |
| A-2 | **Return to the whole world, associated with Overview** (U-2): while Overview is ON and T-08 offers `RETURN_WORLD` (a deeper rung, or WORLD away from the centre), ONE compact chip appears directly under the Overview chip, with T-08's own approved label («العودة إلى العالم كله» / "Return to the whole world"); pressing it is the T-07 `returnWorld` (canonical, recorded in history). When Overview is off, or the act is not offered, nothing is shown. Overview's own behaviour is untouched; the two acts are never combined. At WORLD away from the centre the Back chip remains the same act (§15.3 C), so no second permanent button exists. |
| A-3 | **The remaining T-07 / T-04 acts move into the bottom timeline area** (U-3, U-4): the rail's words row — the row that already holds the Live words — carried them at its START as compact chips in one scrollable line (73c77c4a / 8a7c04ba). **As corrected in §16.6 (a2b8f276):** the acts are a wrapping chip group in their own row ABOVE the label — `BACK_ONE_STEP`, `EXACT_RETURN`, `RETURN_LIVE_FOCUS`, `GO_LIVE_AND_LOCATE` (the same 44-point press targets with the 32-point glass chip; the hint spoken) — present only while acts are offered; the Live words (`RETURN_LIVE_HEAD`) stay alone at the END of the words row, as the accepted rail had them. Everything that comes and goes with the temporal state sits above the segments, so the bottom-anchored rail grows upward and the segments never move. |
| A-4 | **The compact Moment label** (U-6): above the segments the rail draws `timelinePoint(committed)` — the already-approved word of the segments themselves («اللحظة 12» / "Moment 12") — ONLY while a Moment is committed (PINNED); while following Live it draws nothing (the Live words below carry the authorised state «متابعة المحادثة مفعّلة الآن» / "Following the conversation"). The number is T-06's committed Session Position from the canonical store. The T-08 temporal sentence is kept as the label's accessible label (and, during a preview, the preview sentence), so the T-08 meaning stays available to assistive technology while nothing explanatory is drawn. `TemporalOrientationLine` is no longer composed on the Personal screen; T-08's copy file is not edited. |
| A-5 | **Numerals** (U-6 "follow the existing policy"): QANDEEL's policy is Western digits in both languages (`QAN-BL-T12-02`, CLOSED; `analysis-copy.ts`: "Western digits in both languages"), so the Arabic label reads «اللحظة 12», not «اللحظة ١٢». Recorded for the PO in §16.5 R-1. |
| A-6 | **Identical control positions in both languages** (U-9): the stage's chrome no longer mirrors by language. Back LEFT, Overview RIGHT (with its companion under it), the zoom-level rail RIGHT, the G-6 register LEFT, the rail's segments in time order LEFT → RIGHT with the Live words at the RIGHT and the compact acts at the LEFT — in Arabic exactly as in English (the arrangement of the accepted proof's English captures). Arabic words inside the controls keep their own reading direction and typography; the label text aligns to its language (Arabic right-aligned, English left-aligned) within its fixed box. The T-06 marker geometry therefore runs LEFT → RIGHT in both languages (the `rtl` geometry remains available in the code, unused on the Personal screen). |
| A-7 | **No new words**: every drawn label is an already-approved T-05 / T-06 / T-08 / COSMOS word; the Copy Gate stays CLOSED with 0 PROPOSED rows. |
| A-8 | **Validation** (U-8): the chrome tests, the composition / continuity / golden tests and the root contracts are re-anchored to this composition with §16 as the justification (the golden regenerated a second time, for this intended change); Overview, one-level Back, Return World (both homes), Return Live and the acts are verified independently in jest; the HONOR AFTER protocol is re-run on the corrected build in both languages (before = `proof/eecd954f`, after = the new head's folder), with the narrow-width case covered by the jest responsive cases. |

### 16.4 The device observation the first Slice C proof raised (probed; not reproduced; re-proven in the AFTER run)

The first HONOR run (`proof/eecd954f`) showed every rail function working except ONE: a tap on the rail's Live words («العودة لمتابعة المحادثة» / "Rejoin the conversation") did not return to following Live, in either language (`final-run-c.sh` step 04 and `final-run-c2.sh` step 02, the same `input tap` at the words' centre from the accessibility tree), although the same press passes in jest (the store reaches `FOLLOW_LIVE`) and the accessibility tree shows the words as an enabled, clickable button with nothing layered above them.

**Probe (2026-11-16, `apk/cosmosint-probe.apk` = eecd954f + three `console.log` lines, nothing else; `scratchpad/lw/logcat.txt`).** The same sequence — Moment 12 committed by a tap on its segment, then one `input tap` at the words' centre (275, 2541), then four more taps at the words' top, bottom, left and a 350 ms press — reached the press path every time: the rail's root saw the touch (`onTouchStart`), the words' `Pressable` fired `onPressIn` with `liveAvailable = true`, and `commitLiveEdgeIntent` returned `APPLIED` with a `COMMIT_LIVE_EDGE` history entry on the first tap (the accessibility tree then read «متابعة المحادثة مفعّلة الآن», and the T-08 line «أنت عند اللحظة 12.» was gone) and `NO_OP` / `FOLLOW_LIVE` on each later tap, as the kernel specifies while already following. Overview's `onPressIn` fired on both of its taps. So the touch reaches the words, the executor applies, and the stance returns to Live: the earlier observation is **not reproduced** at the same code and the same gesture, and no source-level defect was established. The one structural difference between the words and the chips that always worked — the chips have a glass background, the words none, which under RNGH's Android orchestrator decides whether a handler-less view absorbs a touch — did not change the outcome under the probe, and the words are left as accepted (no background, no new control; C-9).

**Correction of 2026-11-16 (§16.6, fourth pass).** A second pass believed the cause established — the rail's root (a View with only a testID) flattened by Fabric, its children hoisted into the stage's container, `collapsable={false}` as the fix (e64861ad, and that commit's message says so). The flattening was real in the accessibility trees but it was **not the cause**: with the root kept nested the words still fail. §16.6's fourth pass records the bisect that followed and its disposition; the text below is the disposition as it stood before that.

**Disposition (as recorded before the cause was known).** No correction is made on an unestablished cause (a correction that hides nothing must first have something to correct). The AFTER run (`final-run-c3.sh`, run on the §16 head) repeats the exact step — Moment 12 committed, the Live words tapped once at their centre from the accessibility tree — in both languages and records the stance before and after; its result is the Slice C proof of this function (§16.6). If the words ever fail again on a device, the probe's three log points are the way in, and the fix is then a Slice C defect, not a design change. (The first run's pinch-IN captures also used Slice B's screen coordinates, which the full-height stage no longer maps to the green node — a script defect, corrected in `final-run-c2.sh`; the English "Back one level" step had tapped Overview's position — also corrected.)

### 16.5 Recorded for the Product Owner's review (not decisions taken as approved)

1. **R-1 — numerals**: the PO's example «اللحظة ١٢» uses Arabic-Indic digits; the frozen policy is Western digits in both languages, so the screen shows «اللحظة 12». If the PO wants Arabic-Indic digits here, that is a numeral-policy change (QAN-BL-T12-02), not a Slice C edit.
2. **R-2 — the Overview companion** (A-2) is engineering's reading of "a compact secondary / contextual affordance, preferably associated with Overview": one chip, visible only while Overview is on and the act is offered. Alternatives the PO may prefer: a long-press on Overview (undiscoverable, not recommended), or the companion shown whenever the act is offered regardless of Overview.
3. **R-3 — long labels at the bottom**: the T-08 labels are long («العودة إلى المحادثة والانتقال إلى موضعها» ≈ 250 pt). Since e64861ad the acts WRAP in the words row (U-7, §16.6 second pass): a chip that does not fit beside its neighbours starts the next line whole, and a label wider than the row wraps inside its own chip; nothing is clipped, nothing scrolls out of sight. Shorter approved words would need new Copy Gate rows; none are proposed here.
4. **R-4 — the fixed arrangement** (A-6) is the English arrangement of the accepted proof (Back left, Overview right, Live words right). If the PO prefers the Arabic arrangement for both, it is a one-line change.
5. **R-5 — the compact label's alignment**: Arabic right-aligned, English left-aligned within the same box (typography, U-7), which is the one element whose visual position differs by language; if the PO wants it fixed too, it is a style change.
6. **R-6 — the preview**: during a scrub the drawn label keeps the committed Moment (the ring under the finger is the preview, as in the accepted proof); the preview sentence is spoken, not drawn.
7. **R-7 — which "Back" is in the top row**: the PO's message of 2026-11-16 asks for "Back One Step" at the left of Overview's row. Slice C reads this as the stage's Back (one semantic level OUT per press; Return to World at the first rung away from the centre; hidden when it means nothing) — the control whose semantics the same message lists as preserved — and keeps T-07's own `BACK_ONE_STEP` act («الرجوع خطوة واحدة» / "Back one step", the reversal of the latest step) in the bottom acts line with the other acts (U-3). If the PO meant that T-07 chip for the top-left slot, it is a one-line relocation (`RAIL_ACTS` minus it, the stage's `overviewAct`-style seam plus it) — but it would put a second "Back" beside the first.
8. **R-8 — the acts, wrapped (was: the carousel)**: the one-line scroller of 73c77c4a / 8a7c04ba cut the second Arabic chip at the slot's edge on the HONOR — a clipped control, which the PO's message of 2026-11-16 rules out — so e64861ad wraps the acts, and a2b8f276 gives them their own row above the label (so the segments never move). The cost: in Arabic, with two acts offered at once (after a committed Moment away from Live), the acts row is two chip lines tall (the long «العودة إلى المحادثة والانتقال إلى موضعها» wraps inside its chip); in English, one line. The alternatives remain a carousel (a cut chip as the scroll cue) or shorter T-08 labels (a Copy Gate matter); the PO may prefer either.
9. **R-9 — chip sizes**: the top row's two chips are 40 pt; the bottom acts and the Overview companion are 32 pt (secondary). Consistent within each row, deliberately smaller below; the PO may prefer one size everywhere.

### 16.6 The Product Owner's polish of 2026-11-16 (skills; one row; no overlap; the Live words) — applied in 8a7c04ba

**Source.** The PO's message "COSMOS-INT-01 — PO Authorization: Professional UI Skills, Navigation Polish & Laptop Shutdown" (2026-11-16): use the named professional UI skills; Back and Overview in ONE compact row (Back only when meaningful, no reserved row when hidden); reduce clutter; preserve the accepted COSMOS experience, the rail at the RIGHT, the segmented timeline, the compact label («Moment 12», the existing numbering policy); verify the rendered screen, not the JSX; commit and push to the Draft PR; no merge, no Slice D, no #391–#393 changes.

**Skills (installed under `~/.claude/skills`, each with a `PROVENANCE.md`; folders copied unchanged, no script run, nothing added to QANDEEL's dependencies):** `frontend-design` (anthropics/skills @ 1780c5e5, 2026-11-14, Apache-2.0); `expo-native-ui` and `expo-animation` (expo/skills @ dfdebcc6, 2026-11-15, MIT); `vercel-react-native-skills` (vercel-labs/agent-skills @ 1a2c79d4, 2026-10-27, MIT). Their guidance is professional practice, not product requirements (the PO's own words); where it meets the accepted COSMOS design, the design wins. What was taken from them for this change: restraint ("remove one accessory"; the hierarchy carried by structure, not labels), one chip style per row with equal touch targets, `gap` over margins, never `&&` with a falsy value in JSX, a native press target where React Native's falls through, `onLayout` as the one measurement, and the rule that feel is judged on a release build on the device — which is why §16.7 is the proof, not the tests.

**The rendered screens of 73c77c4a (`proof/73c77c4a`, the §16 build) reviewed against that guidance, and what changed in 8a7c04ba:**

| Seen on the HONOR (73c77c4a) | Judgement | 8a7c04ba |
|---|---|---|
| Top row: Overview alone at the right while the reader is at the world's centre; Back at the left as soon as it means something («العالم كله» / "the whole world" after a pan; «← العالم كله» / "← the whole world" one level in); identical places in Arabic and English (`upper.txt`: Back `[39,306]`, Overview `[892–922,306]–[1161,449]`); nothing else above the world; the Overview companion under Overview only while it is on | the PO's row (§16 U-1 / U-2 / U-9); Back's chip was a 14-pt-padded pill and Overview's a 12-pt-padded chip — two styles in one row | ONE chip style for both (40 pt, the same padding, radius, hairline border, 44-pt target); Back keeps standing down (not drawn, `opacity 0`, disabled) when it means nothing, and the row reserves nothing for it |
| The compact label «اللحظة 3» / "Moment 3" above the segments, right-aligned in Arabic and left-aligned in English; nothing while following Live; no "Reading at" anywhere on the screen (the sentence is the label's accessible name; the only "Reading at" text in the tree is T-06's navigator inside the accepted 1 × 1 headless host) | as decided (U-6 / A-4) | unchanged |
| The words row: the acts line's second chip ran UNDER the Live words («…إلى موضعها» cut by «العودة لمتابعة المحادثة») | a defect — the horizontal scroller was not bounded by its slot, and the Live words could shrink | the slot clips (`overflow: hidden`), the scroller shrinks to the slot, the Live words never shrink; a chip that does not fit scrolls into view instead of overlapping (R-8) |
| A tap on the Live words after a scrub (ar) / after a scrub (en) did NOT return to following Live — the same observation as `proof/eecd954f` (§16.4), now in both languages on a second build; every chip with a glass background took its press every time | a defect with one structural difference between the failing and the working targets: the Live words and the "…" marks are the only press targets without a background, and RNGH's Android orchestrator lets a touch over a background-less, handler-less view fall through to the stage's gesture surface beneath, whose tap then cancels the React Native press on release (the probe of §16.4 fired because it happened to win that race) | the Live words and the "…" marks are gesture-handler `Pressable`s: a native handler of their own, innermost, so they win the tap deterministically; same words, same accessibility role / state / label, same executor (`commitLiveEdgeIntent`, `presentation.page`), no visual change; proven in §16.7 |
| The rail (segments, dot, ring), the right depth rail, the register, the nebula, ~60 fps stills, Back one level, Return to World through the companion, memory over three cycles | unchanged from the accepted proof | unchanged |

**Not done, deliberately:** no new control, no new row, no new wording (Copy Gate untouched), no change to the stage's artwork, motion, pinch, double tap or the depth rail, no Slice D.

**Second pass — e64861ad (the HONOR run of 8a7c04ba, `proof/8a7c04ba`, read against the PO's criteria).** Everything above held on the device except two things:

1. **The Live words still did not take their press** — in both languages, in every sequence (tap-first, scrub-first, short scrub, a second tap, a 350 ms press; `scratchpad/lw2/out`), so the "race" hypothesis of the first pass was wrong and the gesture-handler `Pressable` was not the fix. Two instrumented builds of the SAME code (`apk/cosmosint-probe.apk` = eecd954f + logs, `apk/cosmosint-probe2.apk` = 8a7c04ba + the same logs) passed every sequence, including the proof script itself (`scratchpad/lw2/proof-on-probe`); the one structural difference between an instrumented and a plain build was an `onTouchStart` listener on the rail's root. The accessibility trees show the mechanism: in the plain build the rail's root (a `View` with only a testID — it forms a view but not a stacking context) is flattened by Fabric, and its label, headless host, segments and Live words are hoisted into the stage's container (`qandeel-depth-analysis`) as siblings of the root; in the instrumented build the root keeps them as its children. **Attempted fix — refuted by the next run:** `collapsable={false}` on the rail's root keeps its native children (the trees of e64861ad / a2b8f276 confirm it), and the Live words and the marks stay gesture-handler `Pressable`s; the press still fails — see the fourth pass below. e64861ad's commit message ("cause established") is therefore wrong and is corrected here, not rewritten.
2. **The acts line clipped the second Arabic chip** at the slot's edge («…ع إلى موضعها» visible, the rest cut) — no longer under the Live words, but a clipped control, which the PO's criteria rule out. **Fix:** the acts wrap (R-3 / R-8 as rewritten in §16.5): the `layout` prop and the horizontal `ScrollView` are removed from `CosmosContextualActs`; a press target is never wider than its row (`maxWidth: '100%'`), so a long label wraps inside its chip; the slot has a minimum height, not a fixed one, and no `overflow: hidden`.

Re-anchors of the second pass: the §16 A-3 rail test (the slot's `minHeight`, no clipping), the screen golden (regenerated for the wrap layout), the s7-03 / s7-04 pin of `LivingAnalysisMap.tsx` (one line, `layout="line"`, removed). No executor, word or temporal contract changes; no new wording.

**Third pass — a2b8f276 (the HONOR run of e64861ad).** The wrapped acts under the segments made the words row two chip lines tall in Arabic the moment a Moment was committed, which pushed the segments — the surface under the reader's finger — up by a line (and the proof's scrub, aimed where the segments had been, landed on the chips). **Fix:** the acts move to their own row ABOVE the label (`COSMOS_RAIL_ACTS_ROW_TEST_ID`), present only while acts are offered; the words row holds the Live words alone at the END, as the accepted rail had them; the bottom-anchored rail now grows and shrinks upward only. A-3 is restated above. Re-anchors: the A-3 rail test (the acts row is the rail's first child; the words row has no act), the screen golden (regenerated for the new order).

**Fourth pass — the Live-words bisect (2026-11-16, `proof/live-words-bisect/`).** The second pass's claim was premature: e64861ad and a2b8f276 keep the rail's root nested (their accessibility trees show the label, the segments and the words as the root's children) and the words still fail in every sequence in both languages (`live-exp3-e64861ad.log`, `proof/a2b8f276/rail.txt`). The native view hierarchies (`dumpsys activity top`, `top-plain-y-en.txt` against `top-probe-ar.txt`) of a plain and a probe build are identical in shape over the words' centre: the words' button is the top-most touchable view under the point, and the one full-screen sibling drawn above the rail is an empty, backgroundless, non-clickable `ReactViewGroup` that neither React Native's touch system nor the gesture-handler orchestrator treats as a target. So the difference between a plain build (fails) and a probe build (passes) is not structural. The probe's instrumentation was four things: a logging `onTouchStart` arrow on the rail's root, logging `onPressIn` / `onPressOut` arrows on the Live words (all three inline, a new identity every render), a `console.log` inside `goLive`, and a logging `onPressIn` on the Overview chip. Bisect, one release build each on a2b8f276, the same four-sequence experiment (`live-exp3.sh`: tap-first and scrub-first, Arabic then English, fresh coordinates before every tap): **X** = an inline `onTouchStart={() => undefined}` on the rail's root — FAIL 4 / 4 (`live-exp3-x.log`); **Y** = `onPressIn` / `onPressOut` no-ops with a stable module-level identity on the words — FAIL 4 / 4 (`live-exp3-y.log`); **Z** = X together with inline (per-render identity) `onPressIn={() => undefined} onPressOut={() => undefined}` on the words, i.e. the probe's listener set without its log lines — **FAIL 4 / 4** (`live-exp3-z.log`; `apk/cosmosint-z-inline.apk`, sha256 `6d54851f…6fa59`). **FAIL 4 / 4** (`live-exp3-z.log`; `apk/cosmosint-z-inline.apk`, sha256 `6d54851f…6fa59`). No source-level cause is established, so no "fix" ships: a change that cannot be shown to correct anything would only hide the defect. The Live words are recorded **NOT MET on the device** (§16.7) for the PO's decision; the function itself is not lost — the headless navigator's "rejoin" action (C-5), T-07's `GO_LIVE_AND_LOCATE` act in the acts row and the accessible route all reach Live on the same device — but the visible words are a dead control until the cause is found. The recommended next step (not started, not authorised; outside Slice C) is a release build that carries the probe's exact log lines behind a runtime flag that stays off — to learn whether their mere presence in the bundle, and not what they print, changes the outcome — read together with `adb shell getevent` for the touch's delivery and the gesture-handler orchestrator's own native logging; the probe's three log points remain the way in.

## 17. Slice C closure — the Product Owner's final decisions (2026-11-16) and their delivery

### 17.1 Source and standing

The PO's message "COSMOS-INT-01 — Final PO Decisions: Slice C Closure" (2026-11-16) authorises exactly two remaining deliverables: **A**, the six concise act labels it approves, and **B**, a visible "Follow live" button that works. Nothing else is authorised. In substance the message decides:

- **Zoom is CLOSED AND ACCEPTED.** Nothing in the pinch engine, focal calculations, camera transforms, gestures, zoom levels or animation curves changes. No zoom investigation or test campaign runs.
- **The six labels are APPROVED** (table in §17.2). They go into the existing Copy Gate through the canonical copy source. Every action keeps its executor, identity, availability, behaviour and detailed accessibility hint. No actions are combined, no control or row is added, and no broader copy redesign is authorised.
- **The PO does not accept a visible control that looks enabled and does nothing.** The fix must be the smallest independently verified correction, found through a bounded diagnostic protocol. T-06's `commitLiveEdgeIntent` stays unchanged, and the timeline design, camera, zoom, renderer and other navigation systems stay untouched.
- **A mandatory acceptance test** runs on the HONOR with the ordinary, non-instrumented release APK, in Arabic and English, over several cycles, including after a drag or scrub. Headless Jest success alone is insufficient.
- **Preserved:** full-screen COSMOS, Back left and Overview right in one row, the right zoom rail, the segmented timeline, the Moment label, pinch, pan, double tap, semantic zoom, performance, and the privacy, authority and return contracts. Slice D is not begun, Future Improvements item 021 is not reopened, and no unrelated PR is modified. PR #395 stays a Draft, with no merge without the PO's explicit approval.

### 17.4 Deliverable B — "Follow live" works (commit `db1d6e23`; re-anchor `edcdad63`)

**The cause.** The rail's `goLive` was `onOutcome?.(commitLiveEdgeIntent(store, preview))`. A JavaScript optional call does not evaluate its arguments when the callee is `undefined`. The product host (`LivingAnalysisMap`) renders the rail without `onOutcome`, so the Live words never ran T-06's intent on any build of the product. `ReturnControls` already documents this exact trap for its acts.

This explains every earlier observation without exception:

- **Every plain build failed** (eecd954f, 73c77c4a, 8a7c04ba, e64861ad, a2b8f276, edbe1710), whatever the press component: React Native `Pressable`, gesture-handler `Pressable`, gesture-handler `BaseButton`.
- **Every instrumented build passed.** Each probe computed the outcome first, in order to log or record it, and only then called `onOutcome`.
- **Headless Jest passed** because its harness always attaches an observer.
- **The X / Y / Z bisect (§16.6) failed** because it added listeners without touching `goLive`.

**The fix.** The act runs first and the observer is told afterwards: `const outcome = commitLiveEdgeIntent(store, preview); onOutcome?.(outcome);`. `commitLiveEdgeIntent` is unchanged; the semantic command is the same.

**The bounded diagnostic protocol that found it (2026-11-16).** Each step was decided before the next, and the device was observed from outside except in the two trace builds:

| Step | What | Result |
|---|---|---|
| D-1 | Trace build (gesture-handler Pressable + in-memory trace + 500 ms dumper) | 3 / 4. In the failing cycle the Pressable's cancel path ran before its native handler finalised (a real press-in race of that component). Because the probe computed the outcome itself, it could not see the `goLive` defect. |
| Fix build edbe1710 | Words as a gesture-handler `BaseButton`, on the D-1 hypothesis | **0 / 20** on the acceptance test: the hypothesis was refuted. |
| B-2 (no rebuild) | `dumpsys activity top` with a held press; a tap above the bottom system-gesture inset | The native button is PRESSED while held (`...P....`), so the touch reaches it. The upper-edge tap fails the same way, so the system-gesture inset is not the cause. The earlier "Moment 11" failure was a protocol defect: after a scrub the rail windows to Moments 1–9. |
| D-2 | Trace build: native state logs of the button's handler; an in-memory JS record printed only on a later Overview tap | 8 / 8. Natively the handler goes BEGAN → ACTIVE → END on every tap. In JS every event arrives and `onPress` → `goLive` → `APPLIED`. The record's own `goLive` computed the outcome first, exposing the short-circuit. |
| Fix build db1d6e23 | The one-line correction above; no instrumentation | **20 / 20** on the acceptance test (§17.5). |

**Why the words stay a `BaseButton`.** D-1 showed the gesture-handler `Pressable` dropping a press through its own press-in re-render. D-2 shows the `BaseButton` path clean on every tap. The `BaseButton` is therefore kept, but it is not the fix: its plain build without the correction failed 0 / 20.

**Tests.**
- **New regression test.** `cosmos-timeline-rail.test.tsx` mounts the rail with no observer, as the product does, and drives pin → "Follow live" three times in each language. It fails on the old `goLive` (2 of 2) and passes on the new one.
- **VPORT-02 re-anchor.** `vport-02-temporal-orientation-p2-contract` pinned the defective text itself. It now requires the act to run before the observer and forbids the optional-call form. Its intent is unchanged: the Live words are T-06's Live edge intent.

**Corrections to earlier records.** These hypotheses were wrong, and none was the cause:
- the "race with the stage's tap" (§16.6, first pass);
- Fabric flattening the rail's root (§16.6, second pass);
- the "re-render race" (the edbe1710 commit message, never pushed and replaced by `ce56499e` + `db1d6e23`).

`collapsable={false}` on the rail's root stays: it is harmless and outside this closure's two deliverables. §16.6's fourth pass and §16.7's "NOT MET" for the Live words are superseded by this section.

**Latent risk, recorded and not changed.** The "…" marks are still gesture-handler `Pressable`s, the component D-1 caught dropping a press. Changing them is outside the two authorised deliverables. A follow-up is proposed in §17.7.

**Run.** `scripts/accept-follow-live.sh` (`proof/accept-db1d6e23/accept.log`) on the HONOR ALI-NX1. The app is the ordinary release APK, with no logging, probe, debug listener or instrumentation. Per language the run makes a cold open, then:

- 3 cycles: scrub from Moment 1 to 9, then tap "Follow live";

|---|---|---|---|---|

| English ("Follow live") | 5 / 5 | 3 / 3 | 2 / 2 | 10 / 10 |
| **All** | | | | **20 / 20 PASS** |

## 18. Personal target centring after a semantic zoom (PO authorisation, 2026-11-16) — ACCEPTED BY THE PO (2026-11-16, after real-device use; Draft PR #396, NOT MERGED)

**v1.5 — acceptance (PO, 2026-11-16).** The PO's message "COSMOS-INT-01 / Slice D — WP0 Authorization" states that PR #396 is accepted by the PO following real-device testing, and the CP1 authorisation of the same day restates it ("accepted by the PO following real-device use"; its GitHub CI completed green, including the Android and iOS Release builds and the simulator smoke tests, on head `4b4f60da`). The "awaiting the PO's review" status of this section is replaced by **ACCEPTED**. The open items of §18.8 were not decided by that acceptance and stay recorded as they are. PR #396 stays a Draft; no merge without the PO's explicit approval.

### 18.1 Source and standing

The PO's message "COSMOS-INT-01 — PO Authorization: Personal Map Target Centering After Semantic Zoom" (2026-11-16) narrowly reopens one zoom behaviour that §17.1 had closed. It applies to the Personal world only.

**Phase A — the fingers are on the glass.** Focal tracking, the Android pointer-lift correction, gesture continuity, scale response and performance are preserved, and nothing re-centres during the gesture.

**Phase B — after a completed semantic step IN toward an identifiable, authorised element:**
- the target is preserved and glides to the usable visual centre, which allows for the top controls, the bottom timeline and the glass;
- there are no jumps and no snapping;
- the user can zoom further into the same target with no manual pan;
- with no unambiguous target, the finger-anchored step stays exactly as it was.

**Not to be done:**
- no second canonical camera;
- no bypass of `zoomSemanticStep`;
- no change to disclosure, privacy, entitlement or inspection;
- no automatic inspection;
- no target outside the disclosed, authorised geometry;
- no change to the semantic depth levels;
- no change to Back, Overview, temporal navigation or return history.

**Other conditions.**
- **Shared and Public** are unchanged.
- **Proof.** It is mandatory on the HONOR with the plain release APK.
- **Acceptance.** The correction is not accepted until the PO reviews the real-device result. Slice B, the Slice C labels and "Follow live" stay undisturbed.
- **Excluded.** No Slice D, no change to PRs #391–#393, no merge of PRs #394 / #395, and no merge without the PO's explicit approval.

### 18.2 Root cause — measured on the device before any code

The positions were measured on the HONOR with the plain release APK of `db1d6e23`, the Slice C head, in `proof/centre-before/db1d6e23`.
- **Measurement method.** Node cores were read from the captures (`nodes.mjs`: near-white cores, eroded so strands fall away, identity by halo hue). The usable centre was read from the dump: across, the glass centre; down, midway between the Overview chip's bottom and the rail's top.
- **Usable centre on the HONOR.** It is (600, 1378) while following live at WORLD, and (600, 1307) once the rail's acts row shows "Previous step". The camera's own centre is (600, 1469), 91 to 162 px lower.

| Candidate cause | Finding |
|---|---|
| **1. Correct finger anchoring without centring** | **The cause.** A pinch IN on the red Thread at (203, 1072) leaves it under the fingers at every rung. In ar and en it stays **1–10 px** from the fingers and **458–464 px** from the usable centre at THREAD, SESSION, ANALYTICAL_OBJECT and SOURCE_PROVENANCE. The purple Thread on the right behaves the same: 3–8 px from the fingers and 430–433 px from the centre. This is CA-Z1 working as designed. |
| **2. Gesture-focal / released-camera / canonical-camera inconsistency** | **Not present at release.** Stationary pinches put the world point back under the fingers within ≤10 px after a ×8 step, so ≤2 px before it. A moving-midpoint pinch holds the target 14–29 px from the midpoint while the fingers hold. The ×8 step about the fingers then magnifies that by about 8 / 1.4, which gives the 72–197 px seen after release. That lag is a Phase A property. It measures the same on both builds under one protocol (§18.5 C) and is left untouched, as the PO ordered. |
| **3. The semantic level transition** | **It compounds cause 1 for Readings.** A Reading orbits its Thread at a screen-constant radius (`orbitOf(...) * k`, with `k = 1 / rungScale(rung)`), so its world position changes from rung to rung while a Thread's does not. Pinching on an off-centre Reading therefore sends its Thread, and the Reading with it, off the glass at the next rung. On the device, the second pinch on the red's Reading at (258, 1352) gave "Nothing in this region" (`proof/centre-before/A-red-en`). |
| **4. Another reproducible cause** | **Found during the proof of the correction (§18.4):** the current depth word on the right rail blocked a pinch finger. |

**The smallest correct intervention** is therefore centring a sole target, measured where the next rung draws it, inside the one existing step. It is not a coordinate fix: no coordinate defect was found.

### 18.3 The correction (commit `66d64885`)

- **`owner-core.ts`.**
  - `soleHit(list, rung, camera, view, x, y)` returns the ONE hit of the disclosed draw list's current layer that covers the point. It uses the same authorised hit geometry a tap reads, and gives null when no hit or more than one covers it.
  - `aimFixedPoint(canonical, view, target, aim, from, to)` returns the screen point a step must be taken about to draw `target` at `aim`. It is `gestureFixedPoint`'s algebra with a different wanted camera, and gives null when not finite.
  - `CosmosWorldOwner.step` gains an optional `aim`.
- **`owners/personal.ts`.** For a pinch IN with fingers, a released camera and an aim, the step proceeds as follows.
  - **Conditions.** The snapshot is READY, the next rung is held, the fingers rest on a `soleHit` mark, and the next rung's layer draws the same id.
  - **The step.** The point handed to `zoomSemanticStep` is the one that lands that mark, at its next-rung position, on the aim. It is the same one `ZOOM_SEMANTIC`, through the same production authority, with the same focal anchor that CA-Z1 already writes into the act.
  - **What it does not do.** No PAN, no second camera, no inspection, and no change to depth or disclosure.
  - **Otherwise** the point is `gestureFixedPoint`'s, exactly as before. That covers no mark, two marks, a rung not held, a mark not drawn at the next rung, OUT, a double tap and the accessible route.
- **`CosmosWorldView.tsx`.**
  - `usableCentre(glass, controlsBottom, railTop)`: across, the glass centre; down, midway between the top row's bottom and the rail's top.
  - The two edges come from `onLayout` into refs. A layout writes no state and renders nothing.
  - The aim is handed with a pinch only; a double tap passes none.
  - The settle keeps the gesture's anchor (`keepAnchor`), so the target glides on the UI thread while the scale eases, with no snap.
- **Shared / Public.** `createFieldOwner` ignores `aim`; its code is unchanged.
- **Canonical semantics.** The act, its checkpoint and Back are unchanged. For a targeted Personal pinch, only the choice of CA-Z1's anchor point changes, which is the behaviour the PO authorised. No broader camera redesign was needed.

### 18.4 The depth word blocked a pinch finger (commit `17035da9`)

The first HONOR run of the correction (`proof/centre-after/66d64885`) behaved differently by language.
- **Arabic.** The red was centred to the deepest rung.
- **English.** The third pinch was lost: the target was not stepped, and the 53 px shift that followed was a one-finger pan.

**Cause.**
- **Where the word sits.** The usable centre lies in the right rail's third row, and at SESSION the current depth word "THE CONVERSATION" is drawn there, across x ≈ 609–1030 px. A finger landing on that native Text never reached the stage, because the gesture handler treats the Text as a touch target.
- **Control case.** A finger on an empty rail row passed through: A2's pinch at (600, 1378) worked.
- **Arabic.** The shorter «المحادثة» stops short of the centre.

**Fix.** The current depth's bead is `disabled`, so a touch on its word never did anything.
- In the Personal world the word now sits in a `pointerEvents="none"`, `collapsable={false}` View.
- Words, style, layout, accessibility and the beads' presses are unchanged.
- Shared and Public keep the word as it was (§18.8).
- The golden re-nests the 13 words in the wrapper and changes nothing else.

### 18.5 HONOR proof — release APK of `17035da9`, sha256 `a9571cea…5443bf727`, plain build (`proof/centre-after/17035da9`)

Offsets are measured from the usable centre. The BEFORE build is `db1d6e23` (`proof/centre-before/db1d6e23`).

**A and B — off-centre targets and repeated zoom.**

| Case | BEFORE | AFTER |
|---|---|---|
| **A left (red at (203, 1072)), first pinch IN, en / ar** | Stays under the fingers: 460 px off the centre | **(600, 1378)**: on the usable centre measured at the pinch; 71 px off the post-step centre once "Previous step" appears |
| **A right (purple at (996, 1468)), en / ar** | 430 / 433 px | **(599, 1378)**, the same |
| **B repeated pinches on the same target, no pan, to SOURCE_PROVENANCE, en / ar** | 458–464 px at every rung | **1 px** at SESSION, ANALYTICAL_OBJECT and SOURCE_PROVENANCE |
| **B boundary (a pinch IN at the deepest rung)** | Refused; the target stays | Refused; the target stays at **1 px** |

**C — moving midpoint, three repetitions on each build.** One protocol on both builds: purple, fingers drifting (−150, −200) while spreading, with a capture taken while held (`*-C` folders).

| Measure | BEFORE | AFTER |
|---|---|---|
| Target distance from the moving midpoint while held | 14 / 29 / 18 px | 1 / 23 / 22 px — Phase A identical |
| Target after release | 324–386 px off the centre | **(599, 1378)** in 3 / 3 |

**D, E and F.**

| Case | BEFORE | AFTER |
|---|---|---|
| **D empty space, 92 px below the green, outside every 78 px hit** | Green at (604, 832); finger-anchored prediction (599, 824) | **Identical: (604, 832)**. No target was invented. |
| **E refused OUT at WORLD** | Refused; 1 px from the fingers | Identical |
| **E OUT on an off-centre target at THREAD** | 0 px from the fingers | **0 px from the fingers**. No centring. |
| **E Back** (one rung OUT, no focal) | Contracts toward the camera centre: (548, 1421) from (190, 1090) | Contracts the same way: (600, 1448) from (600, 1306). Unchanged. |
| **F Arabic and English** | — | A, B and E-back in both languages, identical results |

**On the E Back row.** In English the tracker could not see the Thread's core at (600, 1448), because the word "READINGS AND FINDINGS" covers it. `_back-crops.png` shows the Thread at the same place in both languages.

**Recorded evidence.**
- Recorded passes, three pinches IN on the red with no pan: `centre-en.mp4` and `centre-ar.mp4`.
- Annotated strips, with the usable centre as a gold cross, the target as a cyan ring and the fingers as a white dot: `_before-red-en.png`, `_after-red-en.png`, `_after-red-ar.png`, `_label-blocker-66d64885.png`, `_right-moving-before-after.png` and `_empty-out-before-after.png`.
- Every position is in `measured.txt`.

### 18.6 Performance

SurfaceFlinger frame intervals were measured on the app layer for each pinch and its settle, in both builds.

| Pinch | BEFORE | AFTER |
|---|---|---|
| WORLD → THREAD → SESSION, SESSION → ANALYTICAL_OBJECT, A right, C, D, E (ar and en) | ≈ 60 fps, p95 16.7 ms, 0 stalls, 0 freezes | The same |
| ANALYTICAL_OBJECT → SOURCE_PROVENANCE and the boundary pinch | 48.8–50.0 fps, p95 33.3 ms, 0 stalls | 46.8–47.2 fps, p95 33.3 ms, 0 stalls |

The slower deep-rung frames are a pre-existing cost of the deep rungs. In the BEFORE build those steps ran with the target at the edge, and in the AFTER build with it centred.

**No new per-frame work.**
- The centring runs once per pinch end: one pass over one layer's hits, plus an algebraic point.
- The two `onLayout` handlers write refs only.
- The canvas, the worklets and the renderer are unchanged.
- Item 021 is not reopened.

### 19.3 Phases and geometry

| Phase | Visible height | Canvas | Content |
|---|---|---|---|
| CLOSED | 0 (mounted, translated off-screen, `pointerEvents="none"`, hidden from accessibility) | full gestures | — |
| PEEK | a compact preview: the content's top, at most the accepted card height (156 pt) plus the bottom inset; PEEK never scrolls | visible and interactive above the sheet; the fields' presentation lift (SD-10) | the top of the world's detail (§19.5) |
| EXPANDED (fields only) | from beneath the host's top band (D-11) to the bottom of the screen, less the keyboard overlap | mounted, `pointerEvents="none"`, hidden from accessibility, camera untouched, drift paused (SD-11) | the complete authorised detail in one scroller; the Public discussion keeps its own scroller |

- **Mounting.** The sheet is a sibling AFTER `CosmosWorldView` inside the host's `stage(glass)`. Both are always mounted and nothing is re-keyed, so the canvas, the world view and the camera are never re-created by a phase change.
- **D-11.** The host's top band, mounted above the stage by the ONE surface, stays visible and interactive in every phase.
- **Overflow (long-list amendment).** When the content does not fit in the PEEK, EXPANDED is available (the handle's approved actions and a drag). When it fits, the PEEK shows all of it, and EXPANDED adds nothing.
- **Direction.** Positions are identical in Arabic and English (U-9); the content keeps its language's writing direction.

### 19.4 Presentation state and triggers

- **Ownership.** The phase is presentation state owned by the host (`useDetailSheet`). It is never written to the canonical store, a controller, history, the recovery record or a per-World anchor. One Reanimated shared value carries the drag; the JS phase is set once at a snap's end. No JavaScript runs per animation frame.
- **Opens the PEEK:**
  - a content change: a new focus; results answered; the section list opened; section-near shown; the field's unavailable or empty state;
  - an explicit reader act on the content's own existing controls (the section control, a submitted search);
  - a stage tap answered `FOCUS` (SD-12).
- **Opens EXPANDED:**
  - a drag or the handle's expand action (only when the content overflows);
  - the Public Discussion entry;
  - a direct entry into a DISCUSSION.
- **Closing / collapsing.** A drag, the handle's actions, the hardware Back or the accessibility escape: **presentation only**.
- **Forced CLOSED.** When the world has nothing to show (the content becomes empty: no focus and no list), the sheet closes as presentation. A deliberate controller Back that releases the focus is one such case.
- **Within the same Experience** (the discussion opened or closed): the current phase is kept.

### 19.5 Content (only existing components, words and acts)

| World | PEEK | EXPANDED |
|---|---|---|
| Public (CP1) | The top of the existing `FieldChrome` content, in its existing priority: the section list › the discussion › the focused Experience's panel (Back, title, section, publisher label, published day, Discussion entry with the human count, themes …) › search results / no results / unavailable › section-near › field unavailable + Retry › empty | The same content in full, scrollable — `ServedPanel` with `PublicTopicContent` (pages, roles), nearby; `PublicDiscussion` (read, load more, reply-to navigation, retries, the composer ONLY when `SERVED && canContribute`); the full section list; all results |
| Shared (CP2, not authorised) | meaning, themes, the panel / field states | + all authorised sources under `sourcesHeading`, the existing Conversation act; no source navigation (SD-2), no count (SD-6) |
| Personal (CP3, delivered — §19.16) | inspection statement, lineage, context choice (its one home, SD-4), "Go to this place" (SD-3) | — (G-7) |

**Long-list amendment (binding).**
- No existing function, result, item or actionable entry may become unreachable because of the compact sheet.
- EXPANDED supports scrolling and selection even when NO Experience is focused.
- The existing Public priority, result ordering, controller state, search scope, authorisation and selection acts are unchanged.
- No data is truncated and no number of results is limited by the sheet.
- No new visible copy is added for expansion (the handle's approved accessibility actions and the existing labels only).
- A result selected from EXPANDED runs the existing `controller.focus(id)` unchanged and transitions to the Experience's PEEK.

**Not added:**
- sources, counts not served, previews, excerpts, names (N4);
- audio, voice, publisher disclosure;
- any production entitlement. The composer stays absent while the server answers `NOT_EVALUATED`; a test fixture alone exercises it.

### 19.6 Dismissal, inspection and Back (supersedes §6.2's Back bullet, §6.4 and §4.4 H6)

1. **Dismissal is presentation only (D-12).** EXPANDED → PEEK and PEEK → CLOSED call no `controller.back()`, clear no focus or inspection, write no canonical camera, create no history entry, change no selected context and trigger no server read. The focused mark stays lit.
2. **Personal inspection lifetime is unchanged.** No canonical act clears `IF_ref`, and Slice D adds none. The inspection persists until another inspection or a T-07 restore (`BACK_ONE_STEP` / `EXACT_RETURN`). The stage's Back is B-1.
3. **Fields.** A deliberate `controller.back()` (the panel's Back, the stage's Back chip, or the hardware key once the sheet is closed) keeps its canonical meaning and is what releases a focus.
4. **Hardware Back — ONE authoritative handler per host.** The host's existing handler consults the sheet first:
   1. EXPANDED → PEEK;
   2. PEEK → CLOSED;
   3. only then the existing navigation:
      - Public: `controller.back()` in its local order (sections › discussion › focus › search › section);
      - Shared: Manage › conversation › `field.back()` › root (CP2);
      - Personal: the Replay player's own handler when open, otherwise `cross('CONVERSATION')` (CP3).

   No sheet registers a listener of its own. React Native calls the most recent listener first, and the host re-registers when its focus changes, which is exactly when the sheet opens. Overview is not part of the hardware order (SD-9).
5. **Re-open** by a tap on the focused / inspected item (SD-12). There is no new chrome control.

### 19.7 Gestures and the snap rule (SD-8, the gesture amendment)

- **A separate view above the canvas.** Touches starting on the sheet never reach the canvas; canvas gestures above it never move it. No `requireExternalGestureToFail` is used, so the canvas gets no added latency. The sheet's background is opaque, so a touch on it can never activate a control beneath.
- **Who drags:**
  - **PEEK:** the whole sheet is the drag surface (`activeOffsetY ±12`, `failOffsetX ±16`, so horizontal motion is never captured; a tap stays a tap).
  - **EXPANDED:** only the handle drags. The content scrolls and is never captured by the sheet pan, so normal discussion scrolling cannot move the sheet.
  - **The Public discussion** keeps its own scroller in every phase. With it on screen, only the handle drags.
- **The snap decision** (pure, unit-tested, `sheet-geometry.ts`). The positions are the phases the world allows (Public `{CLOSED, PEEK, EXPANDED}`; EXPANDED only when it adds content). From the phase the drag started in:
  1. **Deliberate motion.** A travel under **24 pt** never changes the phase, whatever the velocity, so a brief accidental flick changes nothing.
  2. **Distance leads.**
     - Moving to the adjacent position needs **40 %** of the gap (toward EXPANDED, or EXPANDED → PEEK).
     - PEEK → CLOSED needs **50 %**.
     - A drag that passes the adjacent position continues the evaluation from it with the remaining travel, so EXPANDED → CLOSED is possible in one long deliberate drag.
  3. **Velocity only assists a deliberate drag.**
     - With a travel ≥ 24 pt and a release velocity ≥ **600 pt/s** in the same direction, the travel is credited with `min(v × 0.1 s, 25 % of the gap)`. Velocity alone can never cross a threshold that distance has not mostly reached.
     - A release velocity ≥ 600 pt/s against the drag keeps the starting phase.
  4. **Settling.** The spring takes the release velocity, with `overshootClamping` when closing. Under Reduce Motion every move is instant.
- **No new dependency; COSMOS motion untouched.**

### 19.8 Disabled controls are truly disabled (amendment)

The shared `Control` component is not refactored. At every new sheet action boundary (the handle's expand / collapse / close, the escape, the gestures):
- availability is enforced in the handler itself, and an unavailable action executes nothing;
- the visual state and `accessibilityState.disabled` agree with it;
- tests prove an unavailable action causes no controller or store mutation.

The existing entitlement checks (the Public composer's `SERVED && canContribute`, the controller's own guards) are preserved.

### 19.9 Accessibility, language and copy

**Accessibility** (headless only; the PO's 2026-11-08 scope, no TalkBack / VoiceOver proofs):
- the handle is a button with the available expand / collapse / close actions and its `expanded` state;
- `accessibilityViewIsModal` while EXPANDED;
- the stage is hidden from accessibility while EXPANDED (`importantForAccessibility="no-hide-descendants"` / `accessibilityElementsHidden`);
- `onAccessibilityEscape` steps EXPANDED → PEEK → CLOSED;
- the CLOSED sheet is hidden from accessibility.

**Copy Gate (`COSMOS_PRODUCT_COPY_GATE`): three rows, APPROVED (PO, 2026-11-16, SD-7), accessibility only:**

| Key | Arabic | English |
|---|---|---|
| `sheetExpand` | عرض التفاصيل كاملة | Show full details |
| `sheetCollapse` | تصغير التفاصيل | Show fewer details |
| `sheetClose` | إغلاق التفاصيل | Close details |

- The twelve previously approved COSMOS rows are unchanged.
- No new reader-visible wording is approved.
- `moreDetail` / `lessDetail` are never reused for the sheet: they name the zoom.

### 19.10 COSMOS and performance protection

- **Preserved:** the canvas instance, the canonical camera, the renderer, the top band, the cosmic design, the gesture latency, the semantic zoom rail, the timeline and navigation, PR #396's centring, frame pacing.
- **Animation:** UI-thread transforms only; no JavaScript per animation frame; no layout animation.
- **SD-10 lift:** the PEEK's measured visible height becomes the stage glass's bottom inset — what the chrome band's panel did before Slice D. `setEnvelope` records the envelope and reads nothing, and CA-O1's steady envelope keeps the anchor. Nothing is dispatched and no camera is written.
- **SD-11 drift:** paused through the existing `drift` prop while EXPANDED.
- **Reads:** no added server reads per presentation transition, proven by counting transport calls.
- **Untouched:** Skia, COSMOS core, the gesture worklets, the camera authority, the accepted navigation logic.

### 19.11 CP1 — scope, acceptance and gates

**Scope:**

14. **HONOR, plain release APK, ar + en:**

A contract pin moves only with its reason written beside it (this section: D-8 / SD-1, D-12 / SD-5), and the protected property is asserted again on the new elements. No test is weakened. Local failures are compared with the known baseline before being called pre-existing.

The phone's Do Not Disturb was found OFF. The PO chose to proceed without it, with the top band protected: every tap above y 700 px waits for a quiet notification shade, and every interaction first checks that the app has the focus. No Android setting on the phone was changed.

- **Sheet proof re-run** on plain Release APKs `c588616d` (Arabic, then English) and `43348099` (Arabic). PASS:

- **Evidence correction (no results).** The CP1 capture of the no-results state was taken with a focus still held, so the sheet showed that focus's panel, not the state. It is re-proven with no focus on `d3251613` in both languages: «لا شيء في العالم العام يطابق هذا البحث.» / "Nothing in Public World matches this search."
- **The long frame, diagnosed.**
  - On `fd0f2b94` it appeared only on phase-changing moves into PEEK or CLOSED, at the finger's lift (collapse 83 / 100 ms, fast-down 83 ms, close 67 / 83 ms). It never appeared on an expand, a short flick, a reversed drag or PEEK at rest.
  - Cause: the sheet told the host its new phase at `onEnd`. The host's re-render and commit therefore landed while the sheet was still in flight.
- **Narrow correction `7bd4af73`, inside the sheet only.** The new phase reaches the host when the move SETTLES (the spring's `finished`), once per phase. Reduce Motion is unchanged. No scene, camera or gesture authority file is touched. A test proves that nothing is told mid-flight or on an interrupted spring.
- **Retest on plain Release `43348099`** (sha256 `2e7f9694…fe507c`). It is clean: SurfaceFlinger, no dumps.

  | Transition | `fd0f2b94` (stall at release) | `43348099` (stall at rest) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Collapse, round 1 | 83 ms | 117 + 83 ms at settle |
  | Collapse, round 2 | 100 ms | 117 + 83 ms at settle |
  | Close, round 1 | 67 ms | 83 ms |
  | Close, round 2 | 83 ms | none |
  | Fast-down, round 1 | 83 ms | none |
  | Fast-down, round 2 | 83 ms | none |
  | Rapid handle taps, round 1 | 217 ms | 100 ms |
  | Rapid handle taps, round 2 | 133 ms | none |

  A dedicated alternation, three slow collapses and three fast-downs, places the long frame of every EXPANDED → PEEK move about 400–470 ms after the lift. That is the settle, when the sheet is no longer moving: 100–183 ms on collapse, 0–100 ms on fast-down.
- **Result.** The sheet's own motion from lift to rest is now free of the release-moment stall. The commit of EXPANDED → PEEK still costs one long frame, but it now lands when the sheet is at rest.

  That commit also flips the stage: drift resumes, and touch and accessibility return to the world's marks (SD-11). Its cost is not separated between the sheet's content and the COSMOS scene. Separating it needs an instrumented build, and any change there would touch the frozen scene and stage, so it is offered for the PO's decision, not attempted.

  After the run: memory 336.6 MB (`fd0f2b94`: 327.3 MB); PEEK at rest 60.1 fps (`fd0f2b94`: 51.7 fps).

  - `zero-d3251613-*`;

**Closure pass (PO decisions "Final Closure Task, COSMOS-INT-01 Slice D CP1 (#397) + UI-POLISH-01 (#398)", 2026-11-16).** The bottom navigation is accepted as it is and was not touched. On this branch, the CP1-specific corrections:

- **Issue A, the EXPANDED → PEEK stall: one bounded diagnosis.** The method was `atrace` per collapse plus SurfaceFlinger intervals, every sample screenshot-validated as starting from EXPANDED.
  - **Found and fixed in the sheet (`bf8f1b38`).** On `ff926115` the settle's commit re-parented the COSMOS canvas: Fabric `REMOVE 13` / `INSERT 13`, the TextureView's `disconnect` / `connect`, and a new SurfaceTexture. The reason: `CosmosSheetStage` is style-only while PEEK, so Fabric flattened it away, and it became a real view only when EXPANDED gave it `pointerEvents` / `importantForAccessibility`. The fix is `collapsable={false}`, so the stage is one native view in every phase. A jest test asserts it, and the test fails without the prop. The trace on `e2695ece` shows no REMOVE / INSERT and one SurfaceTexture throughout.
  - **What remains is not the sheet's.** Measured under equal conditions (tap-expand, 5 rounds each, every sample validated), the long interval is the same before and after:
    - slow collapse: 200 / 183 / 133 / 150 / 150 ms on `43348099`, against 133 / 150 / 167 / 166 / 166 ms on `e2695ece`;
    - fast-down: 117 / 100 / 117 / 117 / 117 ms, against 117 / 117 / 83 / 117 / 100 ms.
  - **The sheet's own travel is smooth on both builds.** In all 20 samples, the frames from the lift to rest show at most 2 frames over 17 ms, and none over 50 ms.
  - **Where the interval falls.** It starts about 400 ms after the lift, after the 300 ms settle spring, when the sheet is at rest. It has two parts:
    - about 50 ms with nothing to draw, while the host re-renders for the told phase;
    - the first frames of the resumed drift (`drift={!expanded}`). The frozen COSMOS canvas draws them on the UI thread, at 27–38 ms of GPU-bound `eglSwapBuffers` each ("waiting for GPU completion" up to 36–39 ms, against 3–4 ms while EXPANDED with the drift off). They hold back the HWUI frame.
  - **Not perceptible as motion.** The atmosphere drifts at most 2.6 pt/s (veil), and the clock is capped at 0.05 s per frame. The held frame therefore moves the scene by under 0.4 pt, on a screen where nothing else moves.
  - **The exact change that would remove it is in the frozen renderer, so it is not implemented.** Either `cosmos/skia/CosmosCanvas.tsx` resumes the drift without the cold full-cost frames, or the scene's per-frame GPU cost on the HONOR comes down. Both need a PO decision under §19.13.
  - **Reduce Motion:** the drift never resumes (`CosmosCanvas` ignores `drift` under Reduce Motion), and the sheet jumps without a spring. The sheet's Reduce Motion test passes.
- **Issue C (`eeae30ee`), the §19.13 exception above.** `PublicLivingAnalysis.tsx` hosts the discussion as `surface="sheet"`. Jest checks both sides: no card in the sheet, with posts and composer kept (AR / EN); the card exactly as before outside it.
- **Issue E, Arabic glyph clipping, needs no code change.** On the HONOR at rest in EXPANDED, the first text sits 62 px inside the scroller, in both languages. The dots seen earlier are the kasra / tanween of «كافٍ» leaving the scroller's top edge on a partial scroll: normal scroll departure, not stationary clipping.

### 19.16 CP3 — the Personal World Detail Sheet, PEEK only (controlled addendum, PO authorisation 2026-11-17)

**Source.** "QANDEEL — COSMOS-INT-01 Slice D CP3 — Personal World Detail Sheet — Execution Authorization" (PO, 2026-11-17). Baseline: #397 `d3422d88`, #398 `bb8bcedc`.

**Authorised:** CP3 only. Not authorised: CP4, Slice E, Replay, any merge, and any reopening of the typography, bottom navigation, CP1, CP2, renderer performance or cosmetics.

**Branch.** It continues on `cosmos/cosmos-int-01-slice-d` (#397). `cosmos/ui-polish-01` (#398) is kept stacked on it by a `--no-ff` merge.

**Scope.** Integrate CP1's `CosmosDetailSheet` into the Personal World, with PEEK only (no EXPANDED state).
- `integration/composition/LivingAnalysisMap.tsx`: the Personal stage composes the sheet the way Public and Shared do (`CosmosSheetStage` with `expanded={false}`, `useDetailSheet`, `reopeningOwner`). There is no SD-10 lift: the glass, the camera, the semantic zoom and #396's usable centre are untouched.
- `integration/composition/DepthComposition.tsx`: the ONE Analysis Back handler asks the sheet first.
- `cosmos/product/chrome/CosmosContextualActs.tsx`: the narrow `choice?` / `note?` props (§19.13).
- `cosmos/product/sheet/CosmosPeekWords.tsx`: new presentation of the PEEK's words and chips.
- Tests.
- No controller, runtime, API, server, copy or dependency change.

**Content (SD-3, made exact).** The PEEK holds only these, each from its existing owner:
1. **The family word.** The approved `analysisCopy` word: «خيط» / "Thread", «قراءة» / "Reading", or «تركيز ناشئ» / "Emerging focus". It is the stage's own word for the inspected mark. It is taken from T-08's render state where that carries a family, otherwise from the object in the current, fresh scene. An identity this moment does not know (`IDENTITY_UNKNOWN_AT_TC`) is never named.
2. **T-08's inspection statement** (`inspectionSentence`), in role `supporting`.
3. **T-08's lineage** (`contextPathSentence`), in role `metadata`, when there is one.
4. **T-04's context choice.** The same `CosmosContextualActs` instance and executor (`switchContext`), with `acts={[]}` and `note={false}`. This is its ONE functional home: the rail's instance has `choice={false}` (SD-4).
5. **"Go to this place"** («الانتقال إلى هذا الموضع»). It is T-04's `directJump`, with the same request the accessible Map's `direct-jump` sends. It is offered exactly where the Map offers it: the inspected object has ONE locus in the current scene. It is never offered where a context would have to be elected.

Conversation and Replay stay in their top-band places (G-7). They are not drawn in the PEEK, and nothing in it is presented as the object's Replay. No new word, label, link or read.

**Presentation.**
- PEEK covers the timeline rail (SD-4): it reaches at least the rail's top. The rail is never re-mounted. Beneath an open PEEK it is kept in place, hidden, inert (`pointerEvents="none"`) and hidden from accessibility, so its layout and the stage's usable centre are unchanged. Closing reveals it exactly as it was.
- Typography is T-08's roles; the family word is the focused mark's gold; the chips are the contextual chips' (32-point glass chip, 44-point target, U-9 row).
- **Engineering note for the PO's review (not a new rule).** §19.3 says that PEEK never scrolls, and Personal has no EXPANDED state to drag into. So at an enlarged system text size where the PEEK's words exceed the 156-point cap, the words scroll inside the PEEK and the handle still drags. Otherwise text would be clipped and an act made unreachable, which the CP3 authorisation forbids. At the accepted size the PEEK does not scroll; this was measured on the HONOR, see the delivery record.

**Lifecycle (§19.4, §19.6).**
- An inspection opens PEEK.
- A tap that answers FOCUS on the inspected object, or on another one, reopens it (SD-12).
- PEEK → CLOSED by the handle, the drag or hardware Back is presentation only. It leaves the inspection, the selected context, the canonical camera and the history untouched, and reads nothing.
- A context switch or a jump keeps the same subject, so the phase stays PEEK. Another object is a new subject.

**Hardware Back (§19.6.4, SD-5).** `LivingAnalysisMap` registers no listener of its own. It hands the sheet's `back` to `DepthComposition` (`registerSheetBack`). The ONE Analysis handler runs in this order:
1. the Replay player's own listener, while the player is shown (it is the most recent, so it runs first; unchanged);
2. PEEK → CLOSED;
3. `cross('CONVERSATION')`.

Overview's Back is unchanged (SD-9).

**Finding F-1 (pre-existing; reported, NOT fixed; outside CP3).**
- `projection-coordinator.ts` fetches each disclosure as `client.fetchDisclosure(sessionId, { tc, depth })`, without the inspection, and `mapProjectionRequest` carries only (session, tc, depth). Production disclosures therefore carry `inspection: null`.
- T-08 consequently renders every Personal inspection as `INSPECTION_NOT_RESOLVED`: «لا يشمل هذا العرض معاينتك.» / "This view does not cover what you are inspecting."
- No lineage and no context choice can appear in production. This was already true of the rail before CP3, and the golden recorded before CP3 shows it.
- CP3 does not change it. Fixing it needs an inspection-aware read: a runtime and request change, and new reads, which this authorisation excludes.
- What production shows today: the family word, that sentence, and "Go to this place" where it applies.
- The resolved content (statement, lineage, choice) is proven in jest by giving every held disclosure a server-like resolution.
- Suggested disposition, for the PO: admit it to the backlog at CP4 (BG-06), owned by a runtime task (an inspection-aware disclosure request). It is not admitted silently here.
- **Superseded by the PO (2026-11-17, v1.8).** F-1 is not admitted to the backlog and is not a Future Improvement. It is "UNRESOLVED — SLICE E REQUIRED FIX — BLOCKING FINAL COSMOS ACCEPTANCE", recorded in the Slice E acceptance register (§20). The suggestion above is kept for provenance only.

**Re-anchors (§19.12).** Each states the original protected behaviour and keeps asserting it.
- `tests/w1a-01-conversation-core-contract.test.mjs`: the ONE Analysis Back handler now asks the sheet first (`if (sheetBack.current?.() === true) return true;`), then crosses to the Conversation exactly as before. Two new assertions: the sheet is asked in exactly one place, and `LivingAnalysisMap` has no `BackHandler`.
- `composition.test.tsx` T12-A95: the sheet handle is a 28-point row with an 8-point `hitSlop` above and below. Its target is measured as drawn size plus `hitSlop`, which is still at least 44 points. The 44-point floor is unchanged.
- `living-analysis-screen-continuity.test.tsx`: the rail's owners are looked up including hidden elements, because the rail is kept mounted (hidden) under an open PEEK. The same owners and the same instance-continuity checks are asserted.
- `living-analysis-screen-golden.test.tsx` and its golden: regenerated once. All 13 cases are identical to the pre-CP3 golden after stripping exactly the CP3 additions: the sheet stage wrapper, the rail holder and the sheet frame. PEEK content appears only in the `inspection` case. The proof is in `QANDEEL_REPORTS/COSMOS-V5/integration/slice-d/cp3/golden-reanchor-diff.json`.
- `s7-03` and `s7-04` frozen pins of `LivingAnalysisMap.tsx`: previous pin `2eaa9efa…`. The Replay composite and player keep `MapSurface` and the `world=` presentation (G-5), with no new read or route; every other pin is unchanged.

### 19.17 CP4 — Slice D three-world closure (PO authorisation 2026-11-17)

**Source.** "QANDEEL — Slice D CP3 Review & CP4 Authorization" (PO, 2026-11-17). CP3's presentation and interaction are ACCEPTED for roadmap continuation: the HONOR ar / en proof, the PEEK lifecycle, timeline preservation, camera continuity, Back and performance. The PO states that this "is NOT acceptance of missing inspection content" (F-1, §20). Baseline: #397 `faddf233`, #398 `aae101d5`.

**Authorised:** CP4 only, using the approved contract and the existing CP1 – CP3 implementations. Not authorised: Slice E (including F-1, E-1 and E-2), Replay, any merge, any new branch of work, optional polish, and any reopening of the typography, navigation design, animations or renderer optimisation. The Arabic Go-chip placement follows U-9 (no new work). The enlarged-text in-PEEK scroll (§19.16) stays documented as an accessibility-only exception.

**What CP4 changes.** No product code. One static closure test, the canonical backlog (QAN-BL-CW-08), this contract and the locators. The release APK of CP3 (`aadfb88e…`, from `c284fa1d`) is therefore the APK of CP4: `apps/` differs from `c284fa1d` only by the new test file.

#### Three-world audit — one sheet, no missing or duplicated control

`cosmos/product/__tests__/detail-sheet-three-worlds.test.ts` reads the shipped sources and pins the closure, so that a later change cannot quietly break it:
1. There is ONE implementation (`cosmos/product/sheet/`), drawn by exactly the three World hosts: `LivingAnalysisMap` (Personal), `SharedLivingAnalysis` (Shared) and `PublicLivingAnalysis` (Public).
2. G-7: Personal is PEEK only (`allowExpanded={false}`); Shared and Public may expand.
3. SD-5: each host has ONE authoritative hardware Back handler, and it asks the sheet first:
   - Personal: `DepthComposition`'s Analysis handler (`if (sheetBack.current?.() === true) return true; cross('CONVERSATION');`);
   - Shared: `SharedWorldArea`'s handler;
   - Public: `PublicLivingAnalysis`'s own (`sheetBack() || controller.back()`).
   No sheet, and neither the Personal nor the Shared host component, registers a listener of its own.
4. SD-4: the Personal context choice has ONE home. Of the three `CosmosContextualActs` instances (the rail, the PEEK's choice, Overview's companion), exactly one draws the choice: the PEEK's.
5. SD-3 / G-7: the Personal PEEK holds no Conversation or Replay entry.

Negative proof by in-place revert: with the rail's `choice={false}` removed, the test fails ("Expected length: 1, Received length: 2"). Restored, it passes.

  - the guard's focus checks stretch the scripted 0.35 s spacing of these taps to about 1.7 s;
  - so the measured interval is the time the EXPANDED sheet rests between two guarded taps.

## 20. Slice E acceptance register (PO, 2026-11-17) — binding; NOT implemented

**Source.** "QANDEEL — Slice D CP3 Review & CP4 Authorization" (PO, 2026-11-17).

**Mandatory roadmap:** CP4 → Slice E (including F-1, E-1 and E-2) → S8B-07 Replay. There is no separate improvement project, and no additional branch of work.

**Standing.**
- These are Slice E's binding acceptance requirements, in addition to §9's Slice E criteria.
- None is implemented. Slice E is not authorised.
- They are active requirements of COSMOS-INT-01 (BG-01). They are not backlog items, and not Future Improvements.

| ID | Requirement | State |
|---|---|---|
| **F-1** | **Inspection-aware disclosure.** The production projection request does not carry the inspected object (§19.16), so the lineage, the resolved inspection statement and the context choice cannot be shown in the production build. During Slice E, the inspection-aware runtime request must be implemented through the existing authoritative disclosure architecture, with appropriate privacy, caching and contract protections. The resolved content must be demonstrated on the HONOR before final acceptance. Not optional; not a Future Improvement. | **UNRESOLVED — SLICE E REQUIRED FIX — BLOCKING FINAL COSMOS ACCEPTANCE** |
| **E-1** | **Visible speaker attribution.** Personal, Shared and Public conversations clearly identify who authored each displayed message. Existing authoritative speaker information is used and privacy is preserved. The Shared World distinguishes individual participants, the reader and QANDEEL. The Public World keeps its Public Identity model. Mandatory for Slice E; not a CP4 design task. | **REQUIRED FOR SLICE E — NOT IMPLEMENTED** |
| **E-2** | **The PO's hands-on three-world pre-merge gate.** Before the final COSMOS merge, an installable Android Release build holds the Personal World and its live analytical COSMOS map, the Shared World and its map, and the Public World and its map. The PO personally navigates all three Worlds on the HONOR, including empty-world states, inspects their screens, and repeatedly switches between them to judge real transition speed and responsiveness. Cold and warm World transitions and time to actual interactivity are measured. Screenshots, static mockups and Claude's automation do not substitute for the PO's hands-on acceptance. | **REQUIRED BEFORE THE FINAL COSMOS MERGE — NOT RUN** |

**Merge gate.** The final COSMOS integration merge is BLOCKED until:
- F-1 is resolved and demonstrated on the HONOR;
- E-1 is delivered;
- E-2 passes;
- §9's Slice E criteria are met;
- the PO explicitly says «ادمج».

- These corrections belong to Slice E. F-1, E-1 and E-2 (§20), §9's Slice E criteria and the final HONOR hands-on PO gate stay mandatory.
- No merge. No Replay. No change to the frozen artwork.
- Work stops for the PO's review after this first D-1 correction package and its actual iOS simulator proof. No further product change is made before that review.
- **D-1 is NOT closed, and BG-10 is NOT claimed fully enforced** (§21.2, §21.12).
